## Sunday, 10:52 p.m. – Pod People

*by Brian David-Marshall*

It does not get any easier when you play in a Grand Prix. Imagine for a moment that you are Jason Schein, Michael Bonde, Martin Berlin, or Corbett Gray. You have gone undefeated on Day One of a 1045 person Limited Grand Prix but you cannot take a moment to fully appreciate that as you have to worry about getting caught in the crossfire of two players vying for the title of Player of the Year. Owen Turtenwald is clearly the headliner at the table -- which is a hard thing to accomplish when you are sitting next to incoming Hall of Famer Shuhei Nakamura. Owen knows a thing or six about closing out the Swiss rounds of a Grand Prix and Shuhei knows a little something about closing out a Player of the Year race and they could play each other multiple times today -- Owen is already up 1-0 on the weekend having handed Shuhei his first loss yesterday.

Also looming in the pod are Pro Tour Berlin Top 8 competitor -- and rogue deck designer -- Kenny Oberg and upcoming MOCS competitor Ricky Sidher, whom we profiled early in yesterday's coverage. Michael Bonde, who went 9-0 yesterday took the little slip of paper that is used to tell players where they were sitting within the pod as a souvenir of the experience.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ad/56/ad5680dc15e8729f8cb26246a60ed28a2cf17635e3d660db715f1e2a6097aca8.jpg)

*Pod 1*

Ryan Leeper in an Arizona player taking part in his first Grand Prix this weekend -- and to some extent the inspiration for this piece. I spoke to a couple of his friends this morning on the walk over and they mentioned how excited Ryan was to meet the players he had only read about and watched in the coverage. He acquitted himself nicely yesterday with an 8-1 record and was seated at Draft Pod 2 and was passing packs to Pro Tour Paris Top 8 competitor Tom Martell and Hall of Famer Brian Kibler. As someone who follows coverage, Leeper is no doubt familiar with 2010 US National Team member Conrad Kolos and two-time Pro Tour Top 8 competitor Chikara Nakajima.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9e/a0/9ea021e7a19a35b81df4d143a527402c2f321a155e1e4ec6b6072baee712d347.jpg)

*Pod 2*

Players in Pod three have to be cowed by both the sartorial style and impressive resume of former World Champion Katsuhiro Mori who headlines that table after quietly going 8-1 yesterday. In a supporting roles in the pod we have Worlds Top 8 competitor Love Janse and GP Montreal Top 8er Alexander West.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/8b/30/8b30ab0d87b85ce30779dfb0abe40abc3c91427ca0db166de5ba0f601dc0233f.jpg)

*Pod 3*

Another Japanese player's name goes above the title in Pod 4. Former Player of the Year -- and 2011 Player of the Year contender -- Shouta Yasooka has put together a stealthily dominant season with Top 32 finishes in all three Pro Tours as well as three Grand Prix Top 8, highlighted by his win in Kobe. The supporting roles at this table are played by GP Denver Top 8 competitor AJ Sacher, New Zealand's own Gene Brumby, and [Tempered Steel](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tempered+Steel) designer Pascal Maynard.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/08/e0/08e032aa3867286930e9abf26bd9e222052084b0254d08240ca235dfd19601c1.jpg)

*Pod 4*

The pods don't get any easier from there with Hall of Famers Jon Finkel, Jelger Wiegersma, and Alan Comer all looming in the X-2 brackets along with the likes of Player of the year hopeful Luis Scott-Vargas, Rookie of the Year leader Elie Pichon or MOCS competitors Jorg Unfried, Reid Duke, and David Caplan all looking to make a late surge in the draft leg of this event.

## Round 10 Feature Match - Owen Turtenwald vs. Martin Berlin

*by Marc Calderaro*

Owen's empty third pack, that had revealed to him that his Red should probably have been White, was a revelation that came a bit too late. That's ok, though. His black is solid enough to get him through. He has three [Dead Weight](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Weight)s smushed in between a bevy of other removal, and some really late [Walking Corpse](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Walking+Corpse)s were a boon to the pile. His deck might just be random dudes and removal, but that describes a fair amount of good decks in this format. There's a lot to be said for casting things each turn.

The Player of the Year frontrunner is sitting undefeated so far, he's hoping to at least 2-1 this pod on his route to another Grand Prix Top 8 berth. Let's see how he could do against Martin Berlin.

**Game 1**

[Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) and [Vampire Interloper](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vampire+Interloper) started the party early for Turtenwald, and followed by a [Geistflame](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geistflame) on a Berlin's [Typhoid Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Typhoid+Rats) took Berlin down to 16 early, then 9.

Berlin tried to play catch-up. He cast [Victim of Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Victim+of+Night) on Owen's [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) and got a [Mausoleum Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mausoleum+Guard) to help block, but Turtenwald was far from out of gas, and the Interloper was far from not flying.

Berlin had [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites), [Moment of Heroism](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Moment+of+Heroism) and a [Avacynian Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacynian+Priest) which he cast but was swiftly given a large ball and chain attached to his ankles. It was a little too much [Dead Weight](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Weight) for the priest to handle. Berlin sunk to 5 before producing a second Priest, still stuck on four lands.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/fb/2c/fb2caf8eb7270e1c8d96860ccdfba289720f4277ad4da063ff33724cd69a38d6.jpg)

*Martin Berlin*

Turtenwald was stuck on three himself, however his deck seemed to be operating fine without it, though he had a [Galvanic Juggernaut](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Juggernaut) in his hand. Next turn the Interloper, a [Typhoid Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Typhoid+Rats) and [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) came in (the Ghoul trading with the Guard). Berlin got two 1/1 flyers, but also sunk to two life. Turtenwald dropped a post-combat land, upping his count to four, then tapped them all for the Juggy.

Berlin had found his fifth land (a [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township)), and tapped them all to [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) the Guard. Turtenwald didn't mind that Berlin now had four blockers, because his [Crossway Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crossway+Vampire) could nullify one, and then simply flashback the [Geistflame](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geistflame) the next turn. That was the plan, and that's what happened.

Owen Turtenwald 1 – 0 Martin Berlin

Berlin sided in an extra [Moment of Heroism](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Moment+of+Heroism) for a [Smite the Monstrous](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smite+the+Monstrous), properly deducing a low amount of 4-power creatures in Owen's deck.

Turtenwald opted for a [Night Terrors](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Night+Terrors), hoping to nab a couple pesky higher-costed cards before they came down. Specifically the [Mausoleum Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mausoleum+Guard) that could create headaches for Turtenwald's small attackers. Berlin had two of them.

This didn't mean the match was in Berlin's favor of course. "This match-up is bad enough of the play. I'll go first," Berlin opined before presenting his newly shuffled deck.

**Game 2**

I really liked Berlin's keep in this game. A [Typhoid Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Typhoid+Rats), a [Moment of Heroism](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Moment+of+Heroism) and five land. It was risky for sure, but he could take out two creatures, and Berlin had all the colored mana he needed, as his base was a bit shaky. The risk paid off as Turtenwald mulliganed and didn't have a play until turn three. And the [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) that came down were not long for the world. He became a Victim of the Night before he even had a chance in the day.

[Typhoid Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Typhoid+Rats), [Vampire Interloper](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vampire+Interloper) and [Avacynian Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacynian+Priest) all came down in short order and picked at Turtenwald's life total. Turtenwald was now in the catch-up slot. He offered the [Typhoid Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Typhoid+Rats) a [Tribute to Hunger](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tribute+to+Hunger), then used his sideboarded [Night Terrors](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Night+Terrors) to catch the last non-land card in Berlin's hand – the [Moment of Heroism](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Moment+of+Heroism) (he used it so it didn't get exiled). Next, [Dead Weight](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Weight) took out the Interloper, and [Harvest Pyre](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harvest+Pyre) took down the Priest.

Now the board was empty and Turtenwald was still on 10 life.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3b/8b/3b8bbcd0b88ac2cde94ceab26464e18659614349b0c6f63ab72ce2a0ec275992.jpg)

*Owen Turtenwald*

Berlin tried to bring back his Interloper with [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites), but Turtenwald had the [Geistflame](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geistflame) for that. The Rites flashback got a two-power creature the next, to avoid that similar ghostly situation again. And the revived [Avacynian Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacynian+Priest) got in there for one before a second [Dead Weight](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Weight) took it down.

You'd think that all this removal from Owen would come at the cost of relevant creature spells, however, instead, all this removal was coming at the cost of land. He was still on three, with a hand of [Falkenrath Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Noble), [Galvanic Juggernaut](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Juggernaut), [Night Revelers](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Night+Revelers), [Tormented Pariah](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormented+Pariah) and [Crossway Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crossway+Vampire)s awaiting their day in the sun (or in most of their cases, the moon). It was 8-20 and Berlin had refueled with a [Village Bell-Ringer](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Village+Bell-Ringer) and a [Thraben Sentry](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thraben+Sentry). If Turtenwald didn't find that fourth land soon, white humans would plink him to death. In perfectly timed fashion, he found it the next turn and started to climb back.

His [Tormented Pariah](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormented+Pariah) caught a [Smite the Monstrous](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smite+the+Monstrous), but his Noble and [Crossway Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crossway+Vampire) went unanswered. The tables continued to flip when [Dead Weight](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Weight) (number three!) took down the Sentry. After damage and various Noble stackage, the scores became 11-10, and Owen took the point lead for the first time in the game.

When the Revelers came down the next turn, and Berlin's lone Bell-Ringer looked conspicuously like a tasty human, all the creatures turned sideways and Berlin extended his hand.

Owen Turtenwald 2 – 0 Martin Berlin

## Round 11 Feature Match - Melissa DeTora vs. Justin Gary

*by Brian David-Marshall*

Although they live on opposite coasts these days, both Melissa DeTora and Justin Gary saw plenty of each other in the robust New England tournament scene throughout the early parts of their careers. Justin was, of course, one of the big name players of that era with three Pro Tour Top 8s including his historic win where he and YMG teammates Rob Dougherty, and Darwin Kastle all finished 1st - 3rd at Pro Tour Houston playing three different decks.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/27/c0/27c0ddb4fb7f37db8b559e6588ce00c1c0cd5edf625def9fa25af42d126e581f.jpg)

*Melissa DeTora*

Melissa was more of a grinder back then, getting to the PT occasionally and regularly playing in PTQs and GPs. Actually she might be [even more of a grinder now](/en/articles/event-coverage/2011/11-12-2011-grand-prix-san-diego-day-one-coverage) -- despite getting her first Grand Prix Top 8 a couple of weekends ago in Santiago. While the two players shuffled up they reminisced about the glory days of YMG when Melissa noticed that Justin was using an old school YMG die with the red dragon on it to roll for choice.

"One of the stores in Rhode Island actually still has them for sale but nobody buys them because nobody knows what that they are," lamented Melissa.

"They are sitting on a gold mine," clucked Justin who won the die roll. "II will play -- shocking I know."

**Game one**

Justin came out quickly with [Walking Corpse](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Walking+Corpse) followed by [Kessig Wolf](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kessig+Wolf) while Melissa did nothing until a turn three [Chapel Geist](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chapel+Geist). Justin could only attack with the Wolf and Melissa took three -- he followed up with [Skirsdag High Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skirsdag+High+Priest).

Melissa drew blood with the Geist and played a second copy of the 2/3 flier.

"Double Geist..." gulped Justin, who had first picked/first pack a [Travel Preparations](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Travel+Preparations) only to have nothing in those colors come his way at the table -- for obvious reasons. Melissa fell to twelve from Justin's Wolf and he added [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) to the team. Melissa untapped and played [Travel Preparations](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Travel+Preparations) targeting her two fliers.

 

![Travel Preparations](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Travel+Preparations)

"That's a good card," sighed Justin as Melissa tapped another two mana and flashed it back. "That's also a good card." He took 8 in the air from the two fliers. Justin attacked with everyone and Melissa announced:

"I am at four."

"Four is low..." said Justin who played [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) and cast [Bump in the Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bump+in+the+Night).

"That is the three damage thing?" asked Melissa.

"Yeah..."

"I am at one," she said waiting to see if there was anything more coming. When there wasn't she untapped and finished Justin off with [Rally the Peasants](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rally+the+Peasants).

"So close but yet..." grinned Justin who had just taken 20 damage over three turns with only four non-lands doing all the work.

DeTora - 1 Gary - 0

**Game two**

[Cloistered Youth](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloistered+Youth) led off the action for Melissa who was on the draw. [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) came down for Justin -- but he did not look happy. Melissa got her inner Fiend on and attacked him down to 17 before playing [Chapel Geist](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chapel+Geist). Justin had [Victim of Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Victim+of+Night) for the flier and attacked for two.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/10/2a/102a9e0f832aae88101b1b5156f652fea68be1463527c811a7d1bd455ae827da.jpg)

*Justin Gary*

Melissa cracked in for three more and played [Sharpened Pitchfork](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sharpened+Pitchfork) which she equipped to the [Unholy Fiend](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unholy+Fiend). Justin was at 14 and she fell to 16 from the Fiend at the end of her turn. Justin attacked her down to 14 with the [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils), played [Walking Corpse](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Walking+Corpse) and then cast [Bump in the Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bump+in+the+Night) to bring her down to 11.

Melissa played [Clifftop Retreat](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Clifftop+Retreat) and cast [Instigator Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Instigator+Gang) which let her swing for four with the Fiend. She moved the Pitchfork over to the werewolf post combat. Justin's deck was full of removal and [Falter](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falter) type effects and Nightbird Clutches let him get Melissa down to 6. He played the last card from his hand, a [Bloodcrazed Neonate](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodcrazed+Neonate).

Melissa considered her turn. "Do you have any cards?"

Justin held up empty hands but looked down at the flashback spells in his graveyard. "I do have some cards I can cast though...I wouldn't want to misrepresent anything" Melissa attacked with both -- 8 power of attackers -- and Justin ultimately put the Neonate in the path of four points of damage. The score was 6 apiece but Justin had Bump and Clutch looming. Melissa played a post-sideboard [Divine Reckoning](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Divine+Reckoning) -- she has sided out the [Rally the Peasants](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rally+the+Peasants) -- and pointed at her [Instigator Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Instigator+Gang). "I am going to choose this one."

![Riot Devils](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Riot+Devils)

"Good choice," said Justin who was left with [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils). After some debate he ultimately decided to attack and Melissa went to 4 before he played [Skirsdag High Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skirsdag+High+Priest).

Melissa attacked for four and Justin sat back in his chair and thought about what to do and what tricks Missy could have. If he could get just one point through on the next attack he could just win by flashing back Bump.

"How many cards do you have?" he asked

"I have three." -- Justin was empty handed. He picked up the Priest and finally decided to block. Now it was Melissa's turn to think. She played [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) and moved the Pitchfork.

Justin did calculations with his fingers and sighed: "Okay...I...do not have a lot of good choices here. We will attack."

Melissa showed him the [Spidery Grasp](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spidery+Grasp) that crushed his remaining creature.

"I figured you had something like that," nodded Justin. "You win."

"You can write that I flashed back the Bump so that I got her to 1 again," he laughed as the two players packed up for the remaining round with their first draft decks.

Final result: Melissa DeTora won two games to none over Justin Gary.

## Round 12 Feature Match - Ricky Sidher vs. Michael Bonde

*by Marc Calderaro*

Michael Bonde is the current Cinderella story. Coming into the event with no byes and currently undefeated at 11-0, the Danish player is vibrant and bubbling with enthusiasm.

"It would be pretty great to 3-0 the Table 1 Draft pool!" He smirked. "I'm just livin' the dream."

But don't count out Ricky Sidher; he's quite a good Cinderella himself. If he hadn't won a Grand Prix trial last night, he would have been in the same position as Bonde. And now, sitting at 10-0-1 (getting a draw in his first round of play), he's also looking to 3-0 the pod at Table 1 – with Player-of-the-Year-lookin' of Shuhei Nakamura, Owen Turtenwald in the midst.

Game 1

Sidher's [Deranged Assistant](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deranged+Assistant) helped power out a [Slayer of the Wicked](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slayer+of+the+Wicked) (targeting Bonde's [Bloodcrazed Neonate](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodcrazed+Neonate)), but it milled a [Mausoleum Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mausoleum+Guard) in the process. Sidher was ok with that, following up with a [Fiend Hunter](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiend+Hunter) on Bonde's [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) and subsequently sinking Bonde to 11, then 6.

![Deranged Assistant](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Deranged+Assistant)

Bonde had an [Armored Skaab](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armored+Skaab) equipped with a Runechanter's Pike, but continued the life-total slide to 3 on the next attack, and Sidher showed no signs of slowing, casting a post-combat [Stitched Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stitched+Drake).

"How many cards?" Bonde asked.

"Two." A sigh came through the Dane's nose.

"Attack." He turned the Skaab sideways.

"With a 2/4?! [Blasphemous Act](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blasphemous+Act)?" It was indeed. Good call, Sidher.

And though Sidher was able to follow the board sweeper with a second [Stitched Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stitched+Drake), Bonde had an [Into the Maw of Hell](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Maw+of+Hell) to take it out, then a [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) and a [Makeshift Mauler](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mauler), to start the slow climb back. He attacked to make he totals 15-2.

"One card?"

"Yeah," Sidher grunted. He had been reduced to chump-blocking. And the game had been so closely in his reach. The Devils were getting +4/+0 from the Pike and +Flying/+Flying from a [Cobbled Wings](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobbled+Wings) and took Sidher to 9. A couple turns later that was that.

Michael Bonde 1 – 0 Ricky Sidher

Bonde let out an excited sound when he clinched Game 1. Inching closer to 3-0-ing this pod, and inching closer to a Top 8 birth. His happiness was palpable.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c4/af/c4afa769f93ca61a84c869a2694b945cfbe8c82262cce3d988e47b261dcca160.jpg)

*Michael Bonde*

"I thought I had that one for sure," Sidher said. He had been a fair amount more calm than Bonde to begin with, but his mood had dampened a bit with the loss.

**Game 2**

Sidher's [Doomed Traveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doomed+Traveler) was able to get in a few points before staring down a [Selhoff Occultist](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Selhoff+Occultist) and a [Delver of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Delver+of+Secrets).

[Battleground Geist](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Battleground+Geist) followed from Sidher and it was equipped with a [Demonmail Hauberk](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demonmail+Hauberk). Believe it or not, Sidher was perfectly ok with sacrificing a [Doomed Traveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doomed+Traveler) to make a 7/4 flyer and take Bonde down to 11.

Bonde was light on land, but he still had a [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) and a [Bloodcrazed Neonate](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodcrazed+Neonate) to help hold down the fort, if the important fort were on the ground. But when a yet another flyer in [Stitched Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stitched+Drake) came down, the optimism on Bonde's side of the table turned to into mere hope.

"Go, go [Blasphemous Act](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blasphemous+Act)!" Bonde drew his top card, then picked up his cards.

"It wasn't [Blasphemous Act](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blasphemous+Act)."

Michael Bonde 1 – 1 Ricky Sidher

As they shuffled up for the rubber game – the intensity ratcheted up fairly high by these up-and-comers' excitement to be doing so well – Sidher coined, "Man, it's been a long two days. And this day isn't even half over."

"And Worlds is next week." Bonde smiled. His excitement is a bit infectious.

Game 3

The first few turns saw many wings from Bonde – a [Lantern Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lantern+Spirit) and some [Cobbled Wings](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobbled+Wings). The newly cast [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) would be more than happy to pick up some of those, but Sidher sent it away with a [Silent Departure](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silent+Departure) and had a [Deranged Assistant](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deranged+Assistant) and a homicidally flipped [Civilized Scholar](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Civilized+Scholar).

It looked like Bonde was having land troubles yet again. He had out three a few turns later than he should have.

"How many cards in your hand?" Sidher asked.

![Blasphemous Act](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Blasphemous+Act)

"Five. One of them is [Blasphemous Act](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blasphemous+Act)." Bonde suggested his opponent play accordingly. If "accordingly" included flashing back a [Silent Departure](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silent+Departure) to bounce the [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) for a second time and make the life totals 18-11 in Sidher's favor, than he did as such.

The board was [Sturmgeist](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sturmgeist), [Doomed Traveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doomed+Traveler) and [Deranged Assistant](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deranged+Assistant) for Sidher and just the [Riot Devils](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riot+Devils) and [Cobbled Wings](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobbled+Wings). Seems like a decent enough time to wipe the board. Bonde cast the Act that he indeed had. Sidher was prepared though, he followed with the [Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spirit)-producing [Mausoleum Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mausoleum+Guard) joined by the 1/1 [Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spirit) from the Traveler.

Bonde was more prepared though, and starting spilling his cards out onto the battlefield: [Armored Skaab](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armored+Skaab), [Stitched Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stitched+Drake), [Runechanter's Pike](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runechanter%27s+Pike), and [Rakish Heir](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakish+Heir). However, Sidher had another Guard, a [Rebuke](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rebuke) and a [Divine Reckoning](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Divine+Reckoning) awaiting their call of duty in his hand.

Which was now. The call of duty came now. After the dust settled there were three Flying [Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spirit)s and a new Guard, against the flying, 4/4 [Armored Skaab](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armored+Skaab) (thanks to [Cobbled Wings](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobbled+Wings) and Runechanter's Pike) and a fresh, large, [Skaab Goliath](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skaab+Goliath).

"How many cards?" It was Sidher's turn to ask.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/25/fb/25fb1addaa990be83484751b26215ee2bfe7054178c6621de594b6286be7e3c0.jpg)

*Ricky Sidher*

"Three. I have [Into the Maw of Hell](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Maw+of+Hell)." Bonde said matter-of-factly and assertively. He has a habit of saying exactly what's in his hand. It's refreshing really. It makes my job easier.

Like usual, the low-key Sidher paid the comment no mind as he gave [Mausoleum Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mausoleum+Guard) a [Cackling Counterpart](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cackling+Counterpart) and swung in with the team. The score became 8-6, and Sidher's flying army kept growing and growing. He had more than enough mana untapped to flashback the Counterpart at Instant speed whenever he pleased.

"I'm probably going to lose the next turn, so I'm going to go for it." He saddled up the [Skaab Goliath](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skaab+Goliath), cast Nightbird's Clutches on the two untapped flyers and sent in the necessary 8 damage to take out the remaining life total of Sidher.

Bonde nervously eyed Sidher's last card. "Do you have the [Rebuke](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rebuke)?"

Sidher tossed his final card down on the table. Bonde didn't give it a glance and extended his hand, expecting the win. He was ecstatic. He then saw that the thrown down card. It was indeed [Rebuke](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rebuke).

"Oh, you do?!" Bonde sat back down in his chair, despondent. Sidher's flyers came in next turn to finish Bonde off.

"You're crushing my dreams, man." Bonde said as he sat at 11-1, not having his dreams crushed in the least.

Ricky Sidher 2 – 1 Micheal Bonde

## Sunday, 2:00 p.m.: Draft #1 - Owen Turtenwald, Chasin’ It, Kinda

*by Marc Calderaro*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1c/af/1cafc89a6ce90b79be320d7646764b3d1d306df2ce8c8c18306ae13d50691f1a.jpg)

*Owen Turtenwald*

I think it's fair to say that Owen Turtenwald has been having a good year. Dallas, Denver, Singapore, Atlanta, Providence and Santiago are all cities in which Turtenwald now has Grand Prix Top 8s. That's nuts. And all these impressive finishes have vaulted Owen Turtenwald to the top of the standings in the Player of the Year race. Though others are close behind, and Owen seems to think some are more deserving that he is, going into San Francisco, Turtenwald is the one with a crosshair on his back. And you know what? After yet another undefeated Day 1 here at Grand Prix—San Diego, who's surprised by that?

I loomed over Owen's shoulder during his draft at Table 1 (surprise, surprise) to watch how he approached this first *Innistrad* draft of the day. He wound up with a better-than average, straight-forward Red-Black deck. Though it's not a guaranteed 3-0, Owen said, "I'd be really disappointed if I went 1-2." Sounds somewhat in the 2-1 territory to me.

The deck started with a [Galvanic Juggernaut](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Juggernaut) over the more commitment-heavy [Avacynian Priest](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacynian+Priest), [Harvest Pyre](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harvest+Pyre) and a [Kessig Wolf Run](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kessig+Wolf+Run). However, the next couple picks of [Dead Weight](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Weight) over [Stitched Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stitched+Drake), [Geistflame](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geistflame) over [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites), and [Harvest Pyre](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harvest+Pyre) over [Elder Cathar](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Elder+Cathar), Owen solidified the Red and Black.

The first two packs rewarded his decision with things like [Falkenrath Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Noble), two more [Dead Weight](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Weight)s and a couple very late [Walking Corpse](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Walking+Corpse)s. However, that came at the expense of a few good white cards that might have dissuaded the less committed. And that commitment came back to bite Turtenwald in the third pack, just a bit.

"I should've gone White, I think. My Red is pretty mediocre," he said of the final pile. Two [Harvest Pyre](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harvest+Pyre)s, [Geistflame](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geistflame), a [Night Revelers](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Night+Revelers), [Crossway Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crossway+Vampire), [Tormented Pariah](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormented+Pariah) isn't awful, but it certainly wasn't outstanding. "You'd be proud, Conley \[Woods\]. I'm playing [Bump in the Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bump+in+the+Night)."

 

![Bump in the Night](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Bump+in+the+Night)

Conley gave a first-pump as he sat down at the table next to us. "Man, I can't get anyone else on my side with that one." Owen gave a slight look suggesting that, indeed, he was not on Conley's side, and not happy the be currently playing the card.

Another interesting card that made the cut was Runechanter's Pike. "It was the 23rd card for sure, but it's pretty good – even if it's just a [Viridian Claw](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viridian+Claw), it's probably good enough in the format."

So though the deck isn't a knock-down, drag-out winner, sitting undefeated allows Turtenwald to be happy with a 2-1 finish. Unlike many people who barely made the day, needing the 3-0 just to stay in competition.

When the conversation turned to the Player of the Year race, the pragmatic Owen Turtenwald downplayed the honor choosing to talk up others in the race instead.

"I would much rather see Luis \[Scott-Vargas\] or Martin \[Juza\] win it; they've always been in the race and deserve to win it. It just kind of fell into my lap. I think it means more to them." Though that sounds a little humble, there's an understandable practicality behind it. Though Owen has Top-8ed those countless GPs I've previously counted, he's yet to actually win a Grand Prix, and yet to Top-8 a Pro Tour. "The Player of the Year is a pride win; it's bragging rights. That's great and all, but really, I'd like to win a Pro Tour."

Well, Owen could just win out today and clinch his first Grand Prix win. That would probably feel good, though I guess he might just be forced to settle with being Player of the Year. Ho-hum. What a shame.

## Sunday, 2:31 p.m. –Dino-Myte!

*by Marc Calderaro*

So the usual late-season Grand Prix Day 2 activities have been going on: the Player of the Year race is heating up, the Grand Prix field is whittling down, my brain is being extracted by some Stitcher's Apprentice and used for some nefarious purposes during a [Rooftop Storm](https://web.archive.org/web/20111113000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rooftop+Storm). You know, the reg. But as the focus narrows, sometimes it's nice to take a step back and widen the scope from the 1,000+ people who came to compete in the main event. Because there's always plenty of card-playing and brain-melting going on outside the bounds of the 20 or so Day-2 draft tables portioned off, surrounded by judges, and closely scrutinized by players for the format's next big twists.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d2/43/d2436d335957161da75d2bba6f72a4f49dd185ab9fea22768b0b78178b4a4a43.jpg)

*Tyrell Kizer*

Grand Prixes are great excuses to just come and have a fun weekend with some friends and play some awesome **Magic**. There're tons of artists constantly signing and tons of public events constantly starting. There're the two sets of artist playmats being given to the winners of a Modern or Standard tournament, and a big Legacy event (the winnings being packs of Revised – or as I like to call them, Dual-land Wheels of Fortune) just around the bend. Grand Prixes events aren't just for the cutthroat (or cutbrain) competition in the chosen event format. Plenty of **Magic** players drive down, or even fly down, to play in public events and soak in the **Magic**al atmosphere. Just ask Tyrell Kizer and his friends from Reno, Nevada. They all drove ten hours to be here this weekend and most of them aren't even playing in the Grand Prix.

"I'm here for Legacy," Kizer told me. His friend Kristine had just competed in her first tournament ever. "I got my ass kicked." She said. This made me very happy. Because that's what happened when I first competed, and it makes me uncomfortable when people perform better than I did.

"Any large event that's less than a 12-hour drive away, we're there." Which, granted, doesn't cast quite as wide of a net from Nevada as it would in a more geographically condensed locale, but they still get themselves to a great number of large-scale tournaments to participate in the smaller-scale events. And there's plenty of **Magic** events to go around this weekend. Just today, in addition to the Legacy tourney, the Artist-Playmat Standard and the PTQ—Honolulu Sealed, there's a "Modern for Mox", a "Foil Your Standard Deck", and an "Extended for a Foil *Innistrad* Set". This is, of course, is in addition the infinite drafts constantly firing off for those people looking to maximize their Planeswalker Points. I think I just heard them call out "Draft #97."

At each Grand Prix there are streams of events like these, and with the GP reach extending next year, it's highly likely there's one coming to your area. It's even more likely that there's one within a Kizner-endorsed, 12-hour drive. If you haven't taken the time to come out yet, this upcoming year will be a fantastic opportunity. As Kizner is just one of the many people who didn't even consider entering into the main-event area. But just how and why did I decide to talk to the Nevada-based **Magic** player? What made him stand out from the crowd, you ask? Check out this:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/40/3d/403d37409a38fafd393416801a9b6afdfcbca8ce462a625a236166a006de66d7.jpg)

*DINO-MITE!*

KIzner's been going from event to event getting different artist to make Dinosaur sketches on a single playmat. It's called Dino-Myte. It's awesome. Tyrell Kizner's awesome. **Magic** is awesome. End of story.
